     // empty for KEYSTORE\n    private final int iterations;   // 0 for KEYSTORE\n    private final byte[] iv;\n    private final byte[] ciphertext;\n\n    Envelope(KeySource keySource, String alias, byte[] salt, int iterations, byte[] iv, byte[] ciphertext) {\n        ...\n    }\n\n    byte[] header() { ByteArrayOutputStream ... magic, version, keySource.id, aliasLen, alias, saltLen, salt, iterations BE, iv; Simpler: expose getters returning the arrays; internal usage only.\n```\n\nI'll keep getters: `keySource()`, `alias()`, `salt()`, `iterations()`, `iv()`, `ciphertext()`.\n\n**AesGcmCipher.java**\n```java\npackage vault;\n\nimport javax.crypto.AEADBadTagException;\nimport javax.crypto.Cipher;\nimport javax.crypto.SecretKey;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.GCMParameterSpec;\nimport java.io.ByteArrayOutputStream;\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.io.InputStream;\nimport java.io.OutputStream;\nimport java.nio.file.Files;\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\nimport java.security.SecureRandom;\n\npublic final class AesGcmCipher {\n    private static final String TRANSFORMATION = \"AES/GCM/NoPadding\";\n    private static final int TAG_BITS = 128;\n    private static final int STREAM_BUFFER = 1 << 16;\n    private final SecureRandom random;\n\n    public AesGcmCipher(SecureRandom random) { this.random = random; }\n\n    public Envelope seal(SecretKey key, KeySource source, String alias, byte[] salt, int iterations, byte[] plaintext) {\n        byte[] iv = new byte[Envelope.IV_LENGTH];\n        random.nextBytes(iv);\n        Envelope envelope = new Envelope(source, alias, salt, iterations, iv, new byte[0]);\n        try {\n            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION);\n            cipher.init(Cipher.ENCRYPT_MODE, key, new GCMParameterSpec(TAG_BITS, iv), random);\n            cipher.updateAAD(envelope.header());\n            return new Envelope(source, alias, salt, iterations, iv, cipher.doFinal(plaintext));\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n            throw new VaultException(\"encryption failed\", e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    public byte[] open(SecretKey key, Envelope envelope) {\n        // verify key size matches? \n        try {\n            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION);\n            cipher.init(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, key, new GCMParameterSpec(TAG_BITS, envelope.iv()));\n            cipher.updateAAD(envelope.header());\n            return cipher.doFinal(envelope.ciphertext());\n        } catch (AEADBadTagException e) {\n            throw new VaultException(\"authentication failed: ciphertext, header, or key is not authentic\", e);\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) { ... }\n    }\n```\nNote: `cipher.init(..., random)` overload exists: `init(int, Key, AlgorithmParameterSpec, SecureRandom)`. Yes.\n\nFile streaming:\n```java\n    public void sealToFile(SecretKey key, KeySource source, String alias, byte[] salt, int iterations, Path source_, Path target) throws IOException\n```\nImplementation: generate iv, build envelope header, write header to target output, then CipherOutputStream wrapping, copy. Need to write header first then stream. On failure delete partial file. Return Envelope-ish info? Return the Envelope without ciphertext (header only). Fine.\n\n```java\n    public void openToFile(SecretKey key, Envelope env, Path cipherFile, long ciphertextLength, Path target)\n```\nHmm, when we read an envelope from a file, we already loaded full bytes — for streaming we want to avoid loading whole thing. Reading a big file fully is fine for demo but let's do proper streaming for file ops:\n\n`readEnvelopeHeader(InputStream)`: read magic, version, ..., iv → construct Envelope with empty ciphertext, and remember remaining = ciphertext length unknown (until EOF). For decryption streaming, we need to feed all ciphertext bytes to CipherInputStream and doFinal; GCM tag is the last 16 bytes, handled by CipherInputStream (it verifies tag at EOF). If the tag fails, CipherInputStream.close() throws AEADBadTagException... Known Java behavior: `CipherInputStream.read()` throws AEADBadTagException at EOF, and `close()` may throw. Java 8+ throws IOException wrapping AEADBadTagException from `read()` when tag mismatch. Also close() can throw. Need to be careful: partial plaintext written to target before auth failure — a classic pitfall. Security-correct approach: decrypt to temp file and only move to target after successful authentication; or decrypt in-memory for text. To avoid writing unauthenticated plaintext, I'll:\n- decrypt stream into `target + \".pending\"` temp file (same dir, 0600 perms), verify tag on doFinal/close success, then `Files.move(..., ATOMIC_MOVE, REPLACE_EXISTING)`. On failure delete temp and throw.\n\nThat's a genuinely good security property to demonstrate (never release unauthenticated plaintext), and shows understanding. Also for sealToFile: write to temp then atomic move? For encryption, plaintext integrity is fine; still, use temp+move for atomicity. Keep it.\n\nI also want to bind ciphertext length? Historically CipherInputStream swallowed the exception in older versions — pre-8 it silently truncated! In Java 8u?, `CipherInputStream.read` returns -1 and swallows the exception... Let me recall: JDK-6918059 \"CipherInputStream swallows exceptions\" — fixed in JDK 9 (or 8u??). In JDK 9+, `CipherInputStream` throws IOException wrapping the GeneralSecurityException from doFinal. Since we're on 17, that's fine. But to be robust, I should not rely on CipherInputStream alone.\n\nSafer approach: stream manually with `cipher.update(...)` and `cipher.doFinal()` myself, and handle `AEADBadTagException` explicitly. That gives deterministic behavior and avoids the historical swallowing issue. Also, feeding exactly `ciphertextLength` bytes (excluding tag) — no wait, doFinal() with GCM requires the tag to have been passed through update/doFinal. Standard manual pattern: feed all ciphertext bytes (including the 16-byte tag) via update(), then doFinal() verifies tag. So: feed ciphertextLength bytes total (ciphertext+tag) using `cipher.update` for all but last block? Simplest: `cipher.update(chunk)` for each chunk, then `cipher.doFinal()` at the end → GCM decrypt needs the tag available; update passes everything through, buffering the last 16 bytes internally? Actually GCM decrypt buffers the trailing tag only when the tag arrives in the final update... Hmm, GCM implementation in SunJCE handles `update()` with the tag split correctly? There's a known subtlety: GCM decryption requires the tag to be supplied in the final input, and the implementation handles arbitrary chunking since it can't know which update is last; SunJCE's GCM buffers and only treats the last 16 bytes as tag at doFinal. I believe SunJCE GCM handles this: `GCM decryption` — the implementation updates AAD and processes input, retaining the last 16 bytes for the tag during doFinal. Yes — SunJCE's `GCRTransformer`/`GCM Decrypter` retains 16 bytes for the tag: it decrypts all input via update but holds back the last 16 bytes so doFinal can compute/verify the tag. Actually the SunJCE GCM decrypter does: `prepareDoFinal` → uses the input including tag. With streaming updates, `update()` decrypts all supplied bytes, but the decrypter must not decrypt the tag... \n\nLet me look at it from the practical side: the widely recommended pattern for streaming GCM in Java is to feed everything (ciphertext+tag) through `update()` and call `doFinal()` at the end — and SunJCE's GCM decrypter explicitly handles \"tag split across updates\" by holding back the final 16 bytes in a `tag` buffer. I recall `GCM.java` `Decrypt` class: in `update`, it uses `computeBytesWithoutTag` logic, keeping the last 16 bytes for the tag, and it's documented to handle streaming. Yes: com.sun.crypto.provider.GCM$Decrypt.update processes `input.length - tagLen` bytes at most... Hmm, it can't know if more data follows. The real implementation: Decrypt.update keeps a buffer of up to tagLen bytes: it decrypts `total - tagLen` bytes where total is bytes seen so far. That's exactly the right streaming approach. I'm fairly confident modern SunJCE does this (it's how CipherInputStream works over GCM).\n\nSince CipherInputStream uses the same Cipher.update/doFinal path, using it or manual update is equivalent — the only difference is exception propagation. So: use CipherInputStream for the streaming read, then explicitly call `cipher.doFinal()`? No — CipherInputStream already calls doFinal internally; calling it again returns empty and doesn't re-verify. \n\nRobust plan: on JDK 17, `CipherInputStream.read()` throws `IOException` caused by `AEADBadTagException` at EOF; `close()` propagates too. I'll catch IOException and unwrap the cause chain for AEADBadTagException. That covers it deterministically. Additionally, verify integrity by checking that the decryption consumed exactly the expected bytes.\n\nEven more robust and simple: manual cipher.update/doFinal streaming. If SunJCE streaming had the tag-split issue, CipherInputStream would break too. So manual streaming with explicit doFinal and explicit AEADBadTagException catch is the most transparent. I'll do manual streaming — full control, no reliance on CipherInputStream's exception semantics, and I can zero the buffer.\n\nManual streaming decrypt:\n```java\ntry (InputStream in = new BufferedInputStream(Files.newInputStream(sealedFile)); OutputStream out = Files.newOutputStream(temp)) {\n    long skipped = in.skip(n)  // need full skip loop\n    ...\n    long remaining = ciphertextLength; // ct + tag\n    while (remaining > 0) {\n        int chunk = (int) Math.min(buffer.length, remaining);\n        int read = in.read(buffer, 0, chunk);\n        if (read < 0) break;\n        byte[] piece = cipher.update(buffer, 0, read);\n        if (piece != null && piece.length > 0) out.write(piece);\n        remaining -= read;\n    }\n    if (remaining > 0) throw new VaultException(\"sealed file is truncated: \" + remaining + \" bytes missing\");\n    byte[] tail = cipher.doFinal();\n    if (tail != null && tail.length > 0) out.write(tail);\n}\n```\nHmm — wait: with GCM streaming, `cipher.update` on the last chunk will hold back 16 bytes for the tag and `doFinal()` returns the last plaintext minus tag. Handle null → throw.\n\nwipe: `Arrays.fill(encoded,(byte)0)` on the copy we obtained.\n\nKeyVault — write it.\n\n```java\npackage vault;\n\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.io.InputStream;\nimport java.io.OutputStream;\nimport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission;\nimport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions;\nimport java.nio.file.Files;\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\nimport java.nio.file.StandardCopyOption;\nimport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\nimport java.security.KeyStore;\nimport java.security.SecureRandom;\nimport java.util.ArrayList;\nimport java.util.Collections;\nimport java.util.Enumeration;\nimport java.util.List;\nimport java.util.Set;\nimport javax.crypto.KeyGenerator;\nimport javax.crypto.PBE;\n```\n\nFull code:\n\n```java\npublic final class KeyVault implements AutoCloseable {\n\n    public static final String KEYSTORE_TYPE = \"PKCS12\";\n    public static final int KEY_LENGTH_BITS = 256;\n    public static final int PBKDF2_ITERATIONS = 310_000;   // OWASP 2023 floor for PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256\n    public static final int SALT_LENGTH = 16;\n    private static final String PBKDF2_ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n\n    private final Path keystoreFile;\n    private final SecureRandom random;\n    private KeyStore keystore;\n    private char[] password;\n    private boolean closed;\n\n    public KeyVault(Path keystoreFile, char[] keystorePassword, SecureRandom random) {\n        this.keystoreFile = keystoreFile;\n        this.password = keystorePassword;   // ownership transfers; wiped in close()\n        this.random = random;\n        if (keystorePassword.length == 0) throw new VaultException(\"keystore password must not be empty\");\n        loadOrInitialise();\n    }\n```\n\nloadOrInitialise:\n```java\n    private void loadOrInitialise() {\n        try {\n            keystore = KeyStore.getInstance(KEYSTORE_TYPE);\n            if (Files.exists(keystoreFile)) {\n                rejectLoosePermissions();\n                try (InputStream in = Files.newInputStream(keystoreFile)) {\n                    keystore.load(in, password);\n                }\n            } else {\n                keystore.load(null, password);\n                persist();\n            }\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | IOException e) {\n            throw new VaultException(\"unable to open keystore \" + keystoreFile\n                    + \" (wrong password, or the file is not a \" + KEYSTORE_TYPE + \" keystore)\", e);\n        }\n    }\n```\nNote: PKCS12 with a wrong password → IOException \"keystore password was incorrect\" (or AEADBadTagException). Fine.\n\nrejectLoosePermissions: if POSIX view supported and any of GROUP_READ/OTHERS_READ/… set → warn on stderr (not fail? Fail closed is better for a security lib; but on shared systems maybe warn). I'll harden on write and warn on read if world/group readable... Actually a strict implementation refuses to load a key file readable by others. But we also `harden()` on persist, which fixes perms on create. If an attacker could edit perms, they could edit content. I'll warn (stderr) rather than fail, and harden whenever we write. Reasonable and I'll document it.\n\nhardenPermissions:\n```java\n    private void hardenPermissions() {\n        if (!FileSystems.getDefault().supportedFileAttributeViews().contains(\"posix\")) return;\n        try {\n            Files.setPosixFilePermissions(keystoreFile, PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rw-------\"));\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException | IOException e) {\n            System.err.println(\"warning: could not restrict permissions on \" + keystoreFile + \": \" + e.getMessage());\n        }\n    }\n```\n\npersist:\n```java\n    private void persist() {\n        Path temp = null;\n        try {\n            Path parent = keystoreFile.toAbsolutePath().getParent();\n            Files.createDirectories(parent);\n            temp = Files.createTempFile(parent, keystoreFile.getFileName().toString(), \".tmp\");\n            try (OutputStream out = Files.newOutputStream(temp)) {\n                keystore.store(out, password);\n            }\n            Files.move(temp, keystoreFile, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);\n            temp = null;\n            hardenPermissions();\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | IOException e) {\n            throw new VaultException(\"unable to write keystore \" + keystoreFile, e);\n        } finally {\n            if (temp != null) deleteQuietly(temp);\n        }\n    }\n```\nAlso harden the temp file perms (created 600 by createTempFile by default on POSIX: yes, java creates with 600 for temp files). Good.\n\nKey ops:\n\n```java\n    public boolean containsKey(String alias) {\n        ensureOpen();\n        try {\n            return keystore.isKeyEntry(alias);\n        } catch (KeyStoreException e) { throw new VaultException(...); }\n    }\n\n    public SecretKey loadKey(String alias) {\n        ensureOpen();\n        try {\n            KeyStore.ProtectionParameter protection = new KeyStore.PasswordProtection(password);\n            try {\n                KeyStore.Entry entry = keystore.getEntry(alias, protection);\n                if (entry == null) throw new VaultException(\"keystore \" + keystoreFile + \" holds no key under alias \\\"\" + alias + '\"');\n                if (!(entry instanceof KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry)) throw new VaultException(\"alias \\\"\" + alias + \"\\\" is not a secret key entry\");\n                SecretKey stored = ((KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry) entry).getSecretKey();\n                // Copy out of the keystore so the AES bytes outlive the PBE-protected\n                // key material and can be zeroed independently of the keystore cache.\n                byte[] encoded = stored.getEncoded();\n                try {\n                    return new SecretKeySpec(encoded, \"AES\");\n                } finally {\n                    Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n                }\n            } finally {\n                ((KeyStore.PasswordProtection) protection).clearPassword();\n            }\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) { ... }\n    }\n```\nHmm, `stored.getEncoded()` for PKCS12 secret key returns the key bytes (for AES, `getEncoded` returns raw key). Yes.\n\n`generateKey(alias)`:\n```java\n    public SecretKey generateKey(String alias) {\n        ensureOpen();\n        if (containsKey(alias)) throw new VaultException(\"alias \\\"\" + alias + \"\\\" already holds a key; refusing to overwrite it\");\n        try {\n            KeyGenerator generator = KeyGenerator.getInstance(\"AES\");\n            generator.init(KEY_LENGTH_BITS, random);\n            SecretKey key = generator.generateKey();\n            KeyStore.ProtectionParameter protection = new KeyStore.PasswordProtection(password);\n            try {\n                keystore.setEntry(alias, new KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry(key), protection);\n            } finally { ((KeyStore.PasswordProtection) protection).clearPassword(); }\n            persist();\n            return loadKey(alias);\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) { throw new VaultException(\"unable to generate AES-\" + KEY_LENGTH_BITS + \" key for alias \\\"\" + alias + '\"', e); }\n    }\n```\n\n`deriveKey(char[] passphrase, byte[] salt, int iterations)`:\n```java\n    public SecretKey deriveKey(char[] passphrase, byte[] salt, int iterations) {\n        ensureOpen();\n        if (passphrase.length == 0) throw new VaultException(\"passphrase must not be empty\");\n        if (salt.length != SALT_LENGTH) throw new VaultException(\"salt must be \" + SALT_LENGTH + \" bytes\");\n        if (iterations < MIN_ITERATIONS) throw new VaultException(\"iteration count \" + iterations + \" is below the accepted minimum of \" + MIN_ITERATIONS);\n        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(passphrase, salt, iterations, KEY_LENGTH_BITS);\n        try {\n            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(PBKDF2_ALGORITHM);\n            byte[] encoded = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n            try {\n                return new SecretKeySpec(encoded, \"AES\");\n            } finally {\n                Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n            }\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n            throw new VaultException(\"key derivation failed\", e);\n        } finally {\n            spec.clearPassword();\n        }\n    }\n\n    public byte[] newSalt() {\n        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_LENGTH];\n        random.nextBytes(salt);\n        return salt;\n    }\n```\nMIN_ITERATIONS = 100_000.\n\n`aliases()`:\n```java\n    public List<String> aliases() {\n        ensureOpen();\n        try {\n            List<String> names = new ArrayList<>();\n            Enumeration<String> e = keystore.aliases();\n            while (e.hasMoreElements()) names.add(e.nextElement());\n            Collections.sort(names);\n            return names;\n        } catch (KeyStoreException e) { ... }\n    }\n\n    public void deleteKey(String alias) { keystore.deleteEntry(alias); Encryption always uses the newest version; every envelope\n * records the alias it was sealed under, so older payloads stay readable after a\n * rotation and retired keys can be deleted once their data has been re-sealed.\n */\npublic final class SensitiveDataVault implements AutoCloseable {\n\n    public static final String DATA_KEY_PREFIX = \"data-key-v\";\n\n    private final KeyVault keyVault;\n    private final AesGcmCipher cipher;\n    private final SecureRandom random;\n\n    public SensitiveDataVault(KeyVault keyVault, AesGcmCipher cipher, SecureRandom random) { ... }\n\n    public static SensitiveDataVault open(Path keystoreFile, char[] keystorePassword) {\n        SecureRandom random = new SecureRandom();\n        KeyVault keyVault = new KeyVault(keystoreFile, keystorePassword, random);\n        SensitiveDataVault vault = new SensitiveDataVault(keyVault, new AesGcmCipher(random), random);\n        if (vault.currentAlias() == null) {\n            vault.keyVault.generateKey(vault.nextAlias());\n        }\n        return vault;\n    }\n\n    public List<String> dataKeyAliases() {\n        List<String> out = new ArrayList<>();\n        for (String alias : keyVault.aliases()) if (alias.startsWith(DATA_KEY_PREFIX) && isVersion(alias)) out.add(alias);\n        ...sorted numerically\n    }\n\n    public String currentAlias() { list empty → null; return max version alias }\n    public String nextAlias() { \"data-key-v\" + (max+1) }\n    public String rotateDataKey() { String alias = nextAlias(); keyVault.generateKey(alias); return alias; }\n```\n\nencrypt/decrypt:\n```java\n    public byte[] encrypt(byte[] plaintext) {\n        String alias = requireCurrentAlias();\n        Envelope envelope = cipher.seal(keyVault.loadKey(alias), KeySource.KEYSTORE, alias, new byte[0], 0, plaintext);\n        return envelope.toBytes();\n    }\n\n    public byte[] decrypt(byte[] sealed) {\n        Envelope envelope = Envelope.fromBytes(sealed);\n        return cipher.open(resolveKey(envelope), envelope);\n    }\n\n    public String encryptText(String plaintext) {\n        return Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(encrypt(plaintext.getBytes(UTF_8)));\n    }\n\n    public String decryptText(String base64) {\n        byte[] sealed;\n        try { sealed = Base64.getDecoder().decode(base64); } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) { throw new VaultException(\"ciphertext is not valid base64\", e); }\n        return new String(decrypt(sealed), UTF_8);\n    }\n\n    public byte[] encryptWithPassphrase(char[] passphrase, byte[] plaintext) {\n        byte[] salt = keyVault.newSalt();\n        SecretKey key = keyVault.deriveKey(passphrase, salt, KeyVault.PBKDF2_ITERATIONS);\n        try {\n            return cipher.seal(key, KeySource.PASSPHRASE, PASSPHRASE_ALIAS, salt, KeyVault.PBKDF2_ITERATIONS, plaintext).toBytes();\n        } finally { destroy(key); }\n    }\n\n    public byte[] decryptWithPassphrase(char[] passphrase, byte[] sealed) {\n        Envelope envelope = Envelope.fromBytes(sealed);\n        if (envelope.keySource() != KeySource.PASSPHRASE) throw new VaultException(\"payload was sealed with a keystore key, not a passphrase\");\n        SecretKey key = keyVault.deriveKey(passphrase, envelope.salt(), envelope.iterations());\n        try { return cipher.open(key, envelope); } finally { destroy(key); }\n    }\n```\nPASSPHRASE_ALIAS = \"passphrase\" (constant alias for the header). \n\n`resolveKey(envelope)`:\n```java\n    private SecretKey resolveKey(Envelope envelope) {\n        if (envelope.keySource() != KeySource.KEYSTORE) {\n            throw new VaultException(\"payload was sealed with a passphrase-derived key; use decryptWithPassphrase\");\n        }\n        return keyVault.loadKey(envelope.alias());\n    }\n```\n\nFiles:\n```java\n    public void encryptFile(Path plaintextFile, Path sealedFile) {\n        String alias = requireCurrentAlias();\n        cipher.sealToFile(keyVault.loadKey(alias), KeySource.KEYSTORE, alias, new byte[0], 0, plaintextFile, sealedFile);\n    }\n\n    public void decryptFile(Path sealedFile, Path plaintextFile) {\n        Envelope envelope = cipher.readEnvelopeHeader(sealedFile);\n        cipher.openToFile(resolveKey(envelope), sealedFile, envelope, plaintextFile);\n    }\n\n    public void encryptFileWithPassphrase(char[] passphrase, Path plaintextFile, Path sealedFile)\n    public void decryptFileWithPassphrase(char[] passphrase, Path sealedFile, Path plaintextFile)\n```\nMaybe keep passphrase variants only for bytes to limit surface; On non-POSIX filesystems the\n * calls are skipped with a warning path left to the caller, since access control there is\n * handled by ACLs the JDK cannot portably set.\n */\nfinal class FilePermissions {\n\n    private static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY =\n            Set.of(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE);\n    private static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY_DIR =\n            Set.of(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_EXECUTE);\n\n    private FilePermissions() {\n    }\n\n    static boolean isPosix(Path path) {\n        return Files.getFileAttributeView(path, java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFileAttributeView.class,\n                LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS) != null;\n    }\n\n    static void restrictToOwner(Path path) {\n        apply(path, OWNER_ONLY);\n    }\n\n    static void restrictDirectoryToOwner(Path path) {\n        apply(path, OWNER_ONLY_DIR);\n    }\n\n    /** True when the file is readable by group or other, i.e. exposed beyond its owner. */\n    static boolean worldOrGroupReadable(Path path) {\n        try {\n            Set<PosixFilePermission> current = Files.getPosixFilePermissions(path);\n            return current.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_READ)\n                    || current.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_READ)\n                    || current.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_WRITE)\n                    || current.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE);\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException | IOException e) {\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void apply(Path path, Set<PosixFilePermission> permissions) {\n        try {\n            Files.setPosixFilePermissions(path, permissions);\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException | IOException e) {\n            // Filesystem has no POSIX permissions; The store MAC is verified on load, so\n * a swapped or edited keystore is rejected rather than silently used.\n *\n * <h2>What is deliberately NOT done here</h2>\n * <ul>\n *   <li>No key is ever derived from a constant, a word list, or the data being protected.</li>\n *   <li>No key material is logged, printed, or placed in an exception message.</li>\n *   <li>Keys are returned as {@link SecretKey} objects and are validated as AES-256 before\n *       use; Loading verifies the PKCS#12 MAC, so a edited or\n * swapped keystore is rejected instead of being used quietly.\n *\n * <h2>Deliberately avoided</h2>\n * <ul>\n *   <li>No key is derived from a hard-coded constant, a word list, or the data it protects.</li>\n *   <li>No key material appears in a log line, {@code toString()}, or exception message.</li>\n *   <li>{@link #key(String)} hands back a key for a single operation and validates that it is\n *       AES-256; {@link #zero(javax.crypto.SecretKey)} exists so callers can wipe it after use.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <h2>Passphrase-derived keys</h2>\n * Where an operator only has a passphrase, {@link #deriveFromPassphrase} stretches it with\n * PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 at {@value #PBKDF2_ITERATIONS} iterations over a random 128-bit salt.       }\n    }\n\n    /** Convenience for bytes produced by {@code Envelope#toBytes()}. */\n    public byte[] open(byte[] sealed) {\n        try (Opened opened = open(Envelope.fromBytes(sealed))) {\n            return opened.data();\n        }\n    }\n\n    public Envelope sealFile(Path plaintextFile) {\n        return sealFileTo(plaintextFile, siblingWithSuffix(plaintextFile, \".sealed\"));\n }\n        SecretKey key = keyVault.key(defaultAlias);\n        try {\n            cipher.sealToFile(key, KeySource.KEYSTORE, defaultAlias, new byte[0], 0, plaintextFile, sealedFile);\n envelope.iterations()\n                    + \" iterations, salt \" + envelope.salt().length + \" bytes)\";\n        }\n        return \"keystore alias \" + envelope.alias() + \" in \" + KeySource.PASSPHRASE) {\n            try (KeyVault.DerivedKey derived = derive(envelope)) {\n                byte[] raw = derived.key().getEncoded();\n                return new envelope) {\n        char[] passphrase = requirePassphrase();\n        try {\n            return KeyVault.deriveFromPassphrase(passphrase, envelope.salt(), envelope.iterations(), random);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(passphrase, '\\0');\n        }\n    }\n\n    private KeyVault.DerivedKey deriveDefault() {\n        char[] passphrase = requirePassphrase();\n        try {\n Resolution order is:\n *\n * <ol>\n *   <li>{@code VAULT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD} / {@code VAULT_PASSPHRASE} environment variable;</li>\n *   <li>a file named by {@code VAULT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD_FILE} or {@code VAULT_PASSPHRASE_FILE},\n *       which is how a container secret mounted at a path is consumed;</li>\n *   <li>a masked {@link Console} prompt, when attached to a terminal.</li>\n * </ol>\n *\n * <p>None of these are perfectly clean — environment variables leak through {@code /proc/PID/environ}\n * to the same user — so the returned array is the caller's to zero once used.\n */\npublic final class Passwords {\n\n    public static final String ENV_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD = \"VAULT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD\";\n    public static final String ENV_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD_FILE = \"VAULT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD_FILE\";\n    public static final String ENV_PASSPHRASE = \"VAULT_PASSPHRASE\";\n    public static final String ENV_PASSPHRASE_FILE = \"VAULT_PASSPHRASE_FILE\";\n\n    private Passwords() {\n    }\n\n    /** Keystore password, or an empty array when the operator has not supplied one. */\n    public static char[] keystorePassword() {\n        return read(ENV_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD, ENV_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD_FILE, \"Keystore password\");\n    }\n\n    /** Data passphrase, or an empty array when the operator has not supplied one. */\n    public static char[] passphrase() {\n        return read(ENV_PASSPHRASE, ENV_PASSPHRASE_FILE, \"Data passphrase\");\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Requires a non-empty secret, exiting with a clear message when none of the sources has\n     * one. It covers\nround-trips, tamper/wrong-key rejection, key custody, KDF cost enforcement, file streaming,\nrotation, and secret hygiene.\n\n## Library use\n\n```java\nchar[] storePassword = ...;                      // from env, secret file, or prompt\n\ntry (KeyVault keys = KeyVault.open(Path.of(\"app.p12\"), storePassword);\n     DataVault vault = new DataVault(keys, \"app-data\")) {\n\n    vault.ensureDefaultKey();                    // create the key on first run\n\n    Envelope sealed = vault.seal(secret.getBytes(UTF_8));\n    Files.write(Path.of(\"secret.bin\"), sealed.toBytes());\n\n    try (DataVault.Opened opened = vault.open(Envelope.fromBytes(Files.readAllBytes(Path.of(\"secret.bin\"))))) {\n        String clear = new String(opened.data(), UTF_8);\n    }                                        // Opened.close() zeroes the plaintext buffer\n}\n```\n\nFiles are handled without buffering the whole payload:\n\n```java\nvault.sealFileTo(Path.of(\"config.yaml\"), Path.of(\"config.yaml.sealed\"));\nvault.openFile(Path.of(\"config.yaml.sealed\"), Path.of(\"config.yaml.clear\"));\n```\n\nPassphrase-derived keys, when no keystore is available:\n\n```java\ntry (DataVault vault = new DataVault(keys, () -> readPassphraseFromSomewhere())) {\n    Envelope sealed = vault.sealWithPassphrase(payload);   // salt + iterations go in the header\n    vault.open(sealed);                                    // key rebuilt, never stored\n}\n```\n\n## Where the key lives\n\nKey custody is the part worth reviewing, so it is isolated in `KeyVault`.\n\n**Keys are held in a PKCS#12 keystore file.** The keystore password is the single secret an\noperator or secret manager supplies. Details that matter in practice:\n\n- **File permissions are tightened before the first key is written.** The store is created and\n  rewritten `0600`. A key any local user can read is not a secret.\n- **The store is rewritten atomically** through a `.part` staging file, so a crash mid-write\n  leaves the previous working keystore intact rather than a truncated one.\n- **Integrity is verified on load.** PKCS#12 checks its MAC, so an edited or swapped keystore is\n  rejected instead of being used quietly. A wrong password surfaces as a load failure.\n- **Overwriting an existing alias is refused.** Replacing a key in place strands every payload\n  sealed under it; rotation is an explicit, separate operation.\n- **Aliases are validated** against `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,63}`, so a key name cannot\n  become a path.\n- **No key material is ever logged.** `describe()` and `Envelope.toString()` expose aliases and\n  lengths only; `SelfTest` asserts that a key's bytes never appear in the serialized keystore\n  or in any exception message.\n\n**Secrets never come from a command-line argument.** `Passwords` reads, in order: an environment\nvariable (`VAULT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD`), a mounted secret file (`VAULT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD_FILE`),\nthen a masked console prompt. Arguments are world-readable through `/proc`, land in shell\nhistory, and end up in CI logs. The tradeoff is documented in that class: environment variables\nare readable via `/proc/PID/environ` to the same user, so nothing here is perfectly clean.\n\n**Keys are scoped to one operation.** `DataVault` resolves a key, uses it, and drops it rather\nthan caching it for the process lifetime. This is the honest mitigation for Java's inability to\nwipe key material: `KeyVault.zero()` clears the array it is handed, but `SecretKeySpec` keeps a\nprivate copy and returns a fresh clone from `getEncoded()`, so the key object survives — and no\nJava API can scrub copies the JVM already duplicated or pinned. `SelfTest` asserts this\nbehaviour rather than pretending otherwise. Short-lived sessions plus avoiding heap dumps and\n`-XX:+HeapDumpOnOutOfMemoryError` on key-holding processes is the practical control.\n\n**For production**, put the keystore itself (or the wrapped key) in a dedicated store rather\nthan on the app's disk: an HSM or KMS via PKCS#11/JCA provider, a cloud KMS envelope-encryption\nscheme, or at minimum a secret manager that mounts `VAULT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD_FILE`. The\n`KeySource` seam and `KeyVault` are the places to add a provider; `AesGcmCipher` does not\nchange.\n\n## Passphrase-derived keys\n\nWhere only a passphrase exists, `PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256` stretches it at **310,000 iterations**\nover a random 128-bit salt. The salt and iteration count are stored in the envelope header —\nwhich is authenticated — so the key can be rebuilt without ever being stored, and neither cost\nparameter can be quietly lowered to make an old payload readable. Derivation below 100,000\niterations is refused outright. If hardware-backed key storage is available, prefer it:\npassphrase stretching is the fallback, not the ideal.\n\n## Envelope format\n\n`Envelope` serialises the metadata needed to reopen a payload, and GCM authenticates all of it\nas additional authenticated data. Nothing in the header can be edited without opening failing.\n\n```\nmagic   4 B  \"AVL1\"\nversion 1 B\nsource  1 B  KEYSTORE or PASSPHRASE\nalias   1+N  key name (length-prefixed, max 64 B)\nsalt   1+N   PBKDF2 salt (16 B for passphrase keys, absent otherwise)\niter    4 B  PBKDF2 iterations (0 for keystore keys)\niv     12 B  GCM nonce\nbody    ...  ciphertext + 16 B GCM tag\n```\n\nOverhead is 32 B of header plus the 16 B tag for a keystore key.\n\nDesign points:\n\n- **AES-GCM, not CBC.** GCM is authenticated. CBC without a MAC is malleable and invites padding\n  oracles; `AES/CBC/PKCS5Padding` was not an option worth taking.\n- **A fresh 12-byte nonce per seal**, from `SecureRandom`. Reusing a GCM nonce under the same key\n  is catastrophic — it breaks both confidentiality and the tag — so the nonce is generated at\n  seal time and never accepted from a caller.\n- **The key comes from the authenticated header**, not from the arguments at open time. A payload\n  cannot be re-interpreted under a different key after the fact. `SelfTest` covers the\n  specific attack of rewriting the header's alias to point at another *valid, existing* key: the\n  tag rejects it.\n- **Version and provenance are in the authenticated prefix**, so a future format change cannot be\n  smuggled in as a downgrade.\n\n## File handling\n\n- `sealFileTo` streams in 64 KiB chunks; the payload is never fully in memory.\n- `openToFile` decrypts into a staging file that is promoted to the destination **only after the\n  tag verifies**, so a tampered payload never leaves unauthenticated plaintext at the path the\n  caller asked for. The staging file is deleted on failure.\n- Sealed files and recovered plaintext are created owner-only. Recovered plaintext in particular\n  is not left group/world-readable the way a default-umask create would leave it.\n- `rekeyFile` re-seals under a new alias and **leaves the source untouched**, so an interrupted\n  rotation loses nothing. Rotate by re-sealing first and deleting the old key last; the demo's\n  section 5 shows what happens if you get that order wrong.\n\n## CLI\n\n```sh\nexport VAULT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD='...'      # or VAULT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD_FILE=/run/secrets/ks\n\njava -cp build vault.VaultCli init    --keystore app.p12 --alias app-data\necho 'db.password=...' | java -cp build vault.VaultCli seal --keystore app.p12 --out s.bin\njava -cp build vault.VaultCli info    --in s.bin            # provenance, no decryption\njava -cp build vault.VaultCli open    --keystore app.p12 --in s.bin --out clear.bin\njava -cp build vault.VaultCli keygen  --keystore app.p12 --alias app-data-2026\njava -cp build vault.VaultCli rekey   --keystore app.p12 --in s.bin --out s2.bin --alias app-data-2026\njava -cp build vault.VaultCli keys    --keystore app.p12\n```\n\n`seal` without `--out` prints base64 to stdout; `open` without `--in` reads base64 from stdin,\nwhich is the shape you want for storing a sealed credential in a config file or env var.\n\n`seal`/`open`/`keys` use `KeyVault.openExisting` and **refuse to invent a keystore**: a mistyped\n`--keystore` produces \"no keystore at …\" instead of silently generating an empty store and\nreporting a confusing missing-key error. Only `init`/`keygen` create.\n\n## Known limits\n\n- **Secrets are `char[]`, not truly erasable.** See the key-scoping note above; this is a JVM\n  limitation, not something this code works around.\n- **The PKCS#12 password protects the keystore, and its strength is the weakest link.** A weak\n  store password makes the file permission work beside the point.\n- **No key versioning policy or rotation schedule** — `rekey`/`rekeyFile` are the mechanisms, and\n  deciding when to rotate stays an operational call. Payloads sealed under a deleted alias are\n  unrecoverable by design.\n- **GCM's nonce ceiling** is per-key: at ~4 billion seals under one key, rotate. The nonce is\n  random, not a counter, so this is a birthday-bound limit rather than a hard counter.\n- **No padding or format-preserving encryption**; ciphertext is ~48 B larger than the plaintext.\n- **Permissions are POSIX.** On Windows/ACL filesystems the chmod calls are skipped silently and\n  access control must come from the platform.\n- `info` reads only the header, so it reports lengths without holding the whole file — but it\n  does not verify the tag, so its output describes what a file *claims* to be.\n","file_path":"/data/results/qwen/set_a/pilot/2/security/README.md"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","usage":{"input_tokens":110098,"output_tokens":2908,"cache_read_input_tokens":0,"total_tokens":113006}}},{"type":"user","uuid":"e18273a0-8a60-4a79-b01f-d56141b1713c","session_id":"94c59576-7a11-4fdd-8181-f7f6090fd4a7","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_7d2b5254a2be4d30a91fc636","is_error":false,"content":"Successfully created and wrote to new file: /data/results/qwen/set_a/pilot/2/security/README.md."}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"8aaf5c81-c9ea-4f1a-b0b3-b679868a0506","session_id":"94c59576-7a11-4fdd-8181-f7f6090fd4a7","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"8aaf5c81-c9ea-4f1a-b0b3-b679868a0506","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Let me verify a couple of README claims I made that I haven't explicitly tested:\n1. \"vault.ensureDefaultKey()\" exists — yes.\n2.
